H04L9/00	7	{Cryptographic mechanisms or cryptographic} arrangements for secret or secure communications; Network security protocols<br><br><u>NOTE</u><br><br>This group covers: <br>1.1 Cryptographic mechanisms including cryptographic protocols  and cryptographic algorithms, whereby a cryptographic protocol is a distributed cryptographic algorithm defined by a sequence of steps precisely specifying the actions required of two or more entities to achieve specific security objectives (e.g. cryptographic protocol for key agreement), and whereby a cryptographic algorithm is specifying the steps followed by a single entity to achieve specific security objectives (e.g. cryptographic algorithm for symmetric key encryption). <br>1.2 H04L9/00 focuses on cryptographic mechanisms such as encryption schemes, digital signatures, hash functions, random number generation, key management, said cryptographic mechanisms providing information security such as privacy or confidentiality, data integrity, message authentication, entity authentication, authorization, validation, certification, time-stamping, anonymity, revocation, non-repudiation. <br>1.3 H04L9/00 covers also countermeasures against attacks on cryptographic mechanisms. <br>This group does not cover: <br>2.1 Networking architectures or network communication protocols for securing the traffic flowing through data packet networks and providing secure exchanges among applications communicating through data packet networks, which are covered by H04L63/00. Attention is drawn to the Note 1. after group H04L63/00<br>2.2 Security arrangements for protecting computers or computer systems against unauthorised activity, which are covered by G06F21/00<br> In subgroups H04L9/001 -  H04L9/38, the last place priority rule is applied, i.e. at each hierarchical level, in the absence of an indication to the contrary, classification is made in the last appropriate place. wireless access networks H04W)}<br><br><u>NOTE</u><br><br>This group covers: <br>access to a public data network, such as an IP network, for subscribers, i.e. customers of a network service provider, over a wired network. <br>communication of generic types of data between end-user equipments, located typically at the subscriber premises, and an access server, which acts as interface between the access network and the public data network. <br>This group does not cover: <br>wireless access networks, which are covered by H04W<br>optical distribution networks, which are covered by H04Q11/0067<br>bit-level, or PHY layer, processing of data between digital subscriber line equipments, which is covered by H04M11/06<br>design of DSL, digital subscriber line, modems, which is covered by H04M11/06<br>exchange of data related to functionalities of home network appliances between a home network and an external network, which is covered by H04L12/2803<br>management of WDM parameters in optical multiplex systems, which is covered by H04J14/02<br>circuit-switched access networks, which are covered by H04M7/1205<br>access arrangements for providing telephone service in networks other than PSTN/ISDN, which are covered by H04M7/0066<br>In this group the following terms or expressions are used with the meaning indicated: <br>ATM means Asynchronous Transfer Mode <br>LAN means Local Area Network <br>BRAS means Broadband Remote Access Server <br>DSLAM means Digital Subscriber Line Access Multiplexer <br>MSAN means MultiService Access Node <br>DSL means Digital Subscriber Line <br>IP means Internet Protocol <br>WDM means Wavelength Division Multiplexing <br>SDH means Synchronous Digital Hierarchy <br>OTN means Optical Transport Network <br>PSTN means Public Switched Telephone Network <br>ISDN means Integrated Services Digital Network <br>TDM means Time-Division Multiplexing <br>TDMA means Time Division Multiple Access 	H04L12/28	H04L12/28		2501
